     1→The training pipeline has two distinct LoRA training algorithms:
     2→
     3→1) **QLoRA PEFT fine-tuning** (`train_qlora` in `libs/model-training/src/model_training/trainer.py`): it loads a stored coding trajectory, converts the *last successful* generated code into SFT-style chat messages, then fine-tunes PEFT LoRA weights on top of a 4-bit NF4 quantized base model.
     4→
     5→2) **D2L HyperLoRA distillation** (`train_d2l_qwen3` in `libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_train.py`): it trains a hypernetwork that generates LoRA weight tensors from per-layer activations of an *activation_text* prompt, and distills by matching a frozen teacher model’s behavior over an “answer span” inside `teacher_text`.
     6→
     7→Below is the review with dataset creation included.
     8→
     9→## 1. Correctness and logic
    10→
    11→### QLoRA PEFT fine-tuning correctness
    12→- **Dataset derivation is “single-example per trajectory”.**  
    13→  In `train_qlora`, `messages = format_for_sft(trajectory)` and then `Dataset.from_list([{"messages": messages}])`. Because it always wraps the entire trajectory into one record, the dataset size is effectively 1 sample. That makes optimization highly unstable and makes any mismatch in formatting more catastrophic.
    14→- **`format_for_sft()` chooses only the last `tests_passed=True` step.**  
    15→  In `libs/model-training/src/model_training/trajectory.py::format_for_sft`, it does `successful_step = next((s for s in reversed(steps) if s.get("tests_passed")), None)` and uses only that step’s `generated_code` as the assistant output. This is coherent with a “final solution distillation” approach, but it means earlier intermediate solutions never train the adapter.
    16→- **Potential trainer input-schema mismatch (`messages` vs expected text field).**  
    17→  `train_qlora` passes `train_dataset=dataset` with a column named `"messages"` and sets `processing_class=tokenizer`, but does not provide `dataset_text_field` or a formatting function to convert chat messages into a string. Whether this is correct depends on the exact TRL `SFTTrainer` version/behavior—if TRL doesn’t know how to tokenize a list-of-dicts under `"messages"`, training can fail or silently train on empty/incorrect text.
    18→- **Hard-coded LoRA targets may not match the base model.**  
    19→  `build_qlora_config(... target_modules=["q_proj","v_proj"])`. If the base model uses different projection naming, PEFT may attach no LoRA layers (training runs but updates won’t affect the intended modules).
    20→
    21→### D2L HyperLoRA distillation correctness
    22→- **Answer-span masking likely misaligned for causal LM logits.**  
    23→  `_training_step()` computes `answer_start = len(tokenizer(activation_text)["input_ids"])` (token boundary where answer begins *in the combined prompt*). `_compute_kl_ce_loss()` then slices logits as:
    24→  - `s = student_logits[:, answer_start:, :]`
    25→  - `t = teacher_logits[:, answer_start:, :]`
    26→  
    27→  For causal LM training conventions, logits at position `k` typically predict the token at `k+1` (shifted by one). If that shift is not accounted for, the loss is computed on the wrong token indices (often off by one). This also interacts with short answers: the sliced span can become empty.
    28→- **No guard against empty (or fully truncated) answer spans.**  
    29→  If truncation causes `answer_start` to land at/after the effective sequence length, `s`/`t` become empty. Then `functional.cross_entropy(s.reshape(-1, vocab), ...)` can error or produce invalid values.
    30→- **Teacher labels in “CE” term are argmax-based, not token-id-based.**  
    31→  The CE term is:
    32→  - `ce = cross_entropy(student_span, labels = t.argmax(-1))`
    33→  
    34→  That may be intended as “student matches teacher’s hard distribution mode” rather than “student predicts the teacher text tokens”. But if the intended behavior is “predict teacher_text tokens”, this is logically different.
    35→- **Functional LoRA injection correctness depends on module call signatures.**  
    36→  `apply_functional_lora` patches `module.forward` with `patched_forward(x)` that accepts only one positional argument. This works for standard `nn.Linear(input)` usage (and is unit-tested with fake linears), but would break if the target modules are not called with a single tensor argument (less likely for projection linears, but worth validating for the exact base model).
    37→
    38→## 2. Complexity analysis
    39→
    40→### D2L HyperLoRA distillation (`train_d2l_qwen3`)
    41→Let:
    42→- `S` = effective sequence length (`<= max_length`)
    43→- `L` = total transformer layers in the base model
    44→- `L_sel` = number of probed full-attention layers (`len(hc.layer_indices)`, for Qwen3NextConfig this is 12)
    45→- `V` = vocab size
    46→- `T` = `config.num_steps`
    47→
    48→Per training step in `_training_step()` you perform:
    49→1) **Activation extraction pass** (`extract_activations_with_model`): one full forward through the base model with `output_hidden_states=True`, then stack selected layers. Worst-case time is dominated by the base model: roughly `O(F_base(S))` (for attention, worst-case transformers are often `O(L * S^2 * H)`).
    50→   Space is large because hidden states are materialized: up to `O(L * S * H)` transient, plus returned `features` of `O(L_sel * S * H)`.
    51→2) **Hypernet forward** (`hypernet.generate_weights(features, attn_mask, None)`): time at least linear in the activation tensor volume, i.e. `Omega(L_sel * S * H)` plus internal transformer costs in the hypernet. Exact bound depends on `ctx_to_lora` internals.
    52→3) **Teacher forward** (frozen): another base forward `O(F_base(S))`, no gradient storage.
    53→4) **Student forward with functional LoRA**: base forward `O(F_base(S))` plus a small additive cost for each patched linear projection per layer, typically `O(L_sel * S * r * (in+out))`.
    54→
    55→So worst-case per-step time:
    56→- **Worst-case time:** `O( F_base(S) + HyperNetCost(L_sel, S) )` times a constant factor of ~3 base forwards.
    57→- **Worst-case space:** `O(L * S * H)` transient (from hidden state extraction) plus autograd graph memory.
    58→
    59→Overall over `T` steps:
    60→- **Worst-case time:** `O(T * (F_base(S) + HyperNetCost(L_sel, S)))`
    61→- **Worst-case space:** `O(L * S * H)` (peak), dominated by extracted hidden states.
    62→
    63→### QLoRA PEFT fine-tuning (`train_qlora`)
    64→Because the dataset is one example (`Dataset.from_list([{"messages": messages}])`):
    65→- **Time:** roughly `O(epochs * F_base(S))` with backward (a constant-factor of ~2 vs forward in transformers).
    66→- **Space:** `O(L * S * H)` for activations + memory for LoRA parameters/optimizer states (LoRA is small relative to base activations).
    67→
    68→## 3. Edge cases and robustness
    69→
    70→### QLoRA
    71→- **TRL input mismatch risk:** If `SFTTrainer` expects a `"text"` field and you provide `"messages"`, behavior is undefined and may silently be wrong. Add explicit dataset text formatting or `dataset_text_field` where appropriate.
    72→- **Dataset size = 1 + `gradient_accumulation_steps=4`:** depending on TRL batching/epoch semantics, you may do very few or even zero optimizer updates during a short run.
    73→- **Trajectory has no successful step:** `format_for_sft()` returns `[]` and `train_qlora` raises `ValueError`.
    74→- **Missing keys in trajectory:** `format_for_sft` assumes `successful_step["generated_code"]` exists; malformed trajectory JSON will break.
    75→
    76→### D2L
    77→- **`records` may be empty:** In `train_d2l_qwen3`, if `dataset_path` is provided but preprocessing yields zero valid records, `record = records[(step-1)%len(records)]` will crash on modulo by zero.
    78→- **Truncation can break answer spans:** if `teacher_text` truncates before the answer begins, the sliced logits span can be empty.
    79→- **Tokenizer drift between activation/teacher tokenization calls:** answer boundary may be computed from `activation_text` only but applied to logits computed from `teacher_text`. Even small template/special-token changes can shift boundaries.
    80→- **Potential off-by-one in causal LM loss masking:** as described above, current slicing likely targets the wrong token positions.
    81→
    82→### Functional LoRA injection
    83→- **Patch coverage not asserted:** it logs “patched N modules” but does not verify that all `hc.layer_indices` × all expected projection module names were actually patched.
    84→- **Layer index parsing fragility:** `_extract_layer_idx()` assumes the last numeric segment in `module_path` maps cleanly to an index in `layer_indices`. If real module naming differs, you can patch wrong layers.
    85→
    86→## 4. Algorithmic alternatives and optimizations
    87→
    88→### A. Fix causal-LM “answer span” loss alignment (correctness-first)
    89→Instead of slicing logits starting at `answer_start` directly, compute loss indices in a shift-aware way:
    90→- Tokenize `teacher_text` once to get its `input_ids`.
    91→- Compute the token boundary `answer_token_start` in that same tokenization regime (you already do this *conceptually* with activation_text length, but must align shift).
    92→- For causal LM, set `logit_start = answer_token_start - 1` (clamp at 0), then slice logits `[logit_start : ...]`.
    93→- Add a guard: if slice length is 0, skip that record or raise.
    94→
    95→Trade-off: a little more careful indexing and tokenizer work, but it eliminates a high-impact correctness risk and empty-span crashes.
    96→
    97→### B. Cache expensive frozen computations in D2L
    98→Your D2L loop reuses `records[(step-1)%len(records)]`, so many steps repeat the same record.
    99→- Cache `features` (from `extract_activations_with_model`) and/or cache `teacher_logits` for each record index.
   100→- Since teacher forward is under `torch.no_grad()` and base model is eval/frozen, you can cache teacher argmax labels (cheap) and optionally teacher distributions for smaller vocab approximations.
   101→
   102→Trade-off: caching features/logits increases memory. A good compromise is caching only *teacher argmax ids* (for CE) and/or caching activation extraction outputs to avoid one base forward per step.
   103→
   104→### C. Make QLoRA dataset formatting explicit
   105→Convert chat messages to a single training string before constructing the dataset, e.g.:
   106→- apply chat template (`tokenizer.apply_chat_template`) to messages
   107→- store that output under `"text"` (or use TRL’s `formatting_func`)
   108→
   109→Trade-off: you commit to a chat template policy, but you reduce schema ambiguity and silent failures.
   110→
   111→### D. Make QLoRA optimizer steps robust
   112→Given you have 1 sample:
   113→- set `gradient_accumulation_steps=1` (or dynamically reduce it based on dataset length)
   114→- optionally increase dataset size by using multiple successful steps from each trajectory (not just last).
   115→
   116→Trade-off: changes training dynamics, but ensures the adapter actually learns.
   117→
   118→## 5. Focused improvement checklist
   119→
   120→1. **Make D2L loss slicing shift-aware and non-empty-span safe** in `_compute_kl_ce_loss()` (fix likely off-by-one + avoid empty slices).
   121→2. **Guard D2L against `records == []`** early in `train_d2l_qwen3()` and validate that tokenized `teacher_text` still contains the answer boundary.
   122→3. **Fix QLoRA dataset schema for TRL**: explicitly provide a text field or formatting function so `SFTTrainer` consumes the chat messages correctly.
   123→4. **Adjust QLoRA `gradient_accumulation_steps` for dataset size** (dataset is currently 1 sample per run).
   124→5. **Assert patch coverage in `apply_functional_lora`** (expected patched module count given `target_modules` × `layer_indices`) to prevent silent under-patching.
   125→
   126→
```

     1→# LoRA Training Subsystem — Algorithmic Review
     2→
     3→**Date:** 2026-03-23
     4→**Scope:** Full LoRA training pipeline — hypernetwork generation, QLoRA fine-tuning, TIES/DARE merging, evolution loop, adapter registry, training data sourcing
     5→**Reviewer focus:** Correctness, complexity, edge cases, data provenance, algorithmic alternatives
     6→
     7→---
     8→
     9→## What the system is trying to do
    10→
    11→Rune encodes coding session trajectories into LoRA adapters that provide persistent episodic memory for a code-generation agent. There are **two distinct adapter-generation paths**:
    12→
    13→1. **Instant hypernetwork generation** (Doc-to-LoRA / Sakana HyperLoRA): A frozen perceiver cross-attends over per-layer base-model activations extracted from trajectory text, producing LoRA A/B weight matrices in a single forward pass (<1s). Used **within** the pipeline retry loop to inject per-subtask context without prompt stuffing.
    14→
    15→2. **QLoRA gradient descent** (SFTTrainer): Standard PEFT fine-tuning on NF4-quantized base model using a single successful trajectory formatted as SFT chat messages. Used for **post-session** distillation into the adapter registry.
    16→
    17→An **evolution layer** (TIES/DARE merging + pruning) periodically combines top-fitness adapters per task type, and a **registry** (SQLite + SQLModel) tracks adapter lineage.
    18→
    19→---
    20→
    21→## 1. Correctness and Logic
    22→
    23→### 1.1 Hypernetwork adapter generation (sakana_d2l.py)
    24→
    25→**Alpha-to-PEFT scaling conversion (lines 661–670)**
    26→
    27→The conversion formula is:
    28→```
    29→peft_alpha = checkpoint_alpha * actual_rank * scaling_factor
    30→```
    31→
    32→This compensates for the fact that Sakana uses `scaling = lora_alpha` directly, while PEFT uses `alpha / r`. The math checks out: PEFT applies `alpha/r` internally, so setting `peft_alpha = original_alpha * r` recovers the original scaling, then `* scaling_factor` attenuates. **Correct.**
    33→
    34→However, the fallback when `hc is None` (line 668) uses `actual_rank * 2` as `checkpoint_alpha`. This path is reachable only if `hc` is `None`, which contradicts the function signature where `hc` is always passed from `generate_adapter_from_sakana`. The `if hc is not None` guard on line 667 is dead code (hc is never None at that callsite). Not a bug, but misleading.
    35→
    36→**combine_lora bias concatenation (lines 572–576)**
    37→
    38→`n_chunks = torch.ones(1, dtype=torch.int32)` — this hardcodes single-chunk mode. If the checkpoint was trained with multi-chunk bias, this would silently produce wrong shapes. **Works for the current checkpoint variant (`gemma_demo`) but is fragile** if new variants use multi-chunk.
    39→
    40→**Redundant checkpoint loading (lines 544–549)**
    41→
    42→When `base_model_name is None`, the checkpoint is loaded a second time just to read `base_model_name_or_path`. This is already loaded inside `load_sakana_checkpoint` on line 541. The entire checkpoint dict is deserialized twice. Not a correctness bug but a wasteful pattern (~hundreds of MB).
    43→
    44→**`_save_sakana_adapter` module path prefix (lines 633–634, 644)**
    45→
    46→The module path prefix (`self_attn` vs `mlp`) is selected via a hardcoded set:
    47→```python
    48→_attn_modules = {"q_proj", "k_proj", "v_proj", "o_proj", "qkv_proj"}
    49→```
    50→Any module not in this set gets `mlp` prefix. If Sakana's checkpoint targets modules like `gate_proj` or `up_proj` (which are MLP modules), this is correct. But if a new checkpoint variant targets a module outside both categories, the prefix assignment silently produces a wrong PEFT key. **No validation that keys actually match the base model.**
    51→
    52→### 1.2 Custom hypernetwork (hypernetwork.py)
    53→
    54→**trajectory_to_tokens hash collision rate (lines 286–294)**
    55→
    56→The tokenizer uses `zlib.crc32(trigram) % vocab_size` with default `vocab_size=32000`. CRC32 is 32-bit → birthday paradox gives ~50% collision probability at ~7.7k unique trigrams. A 2048-char document produces ~2046 trigrams. With only ~50³ possible ASCII trigrams (~125k), the effective collision rate against vocab_size=32000 is high.
    57→
    58→**This means semantically different trajectory texts can produce identical token sequences**, making the hypernetwork unable to distinguish them. For short or similar trajectories (common in retry loops where only the error message changes), this is a real risk.
    59→
    60→**forward() always returns batch element 0 (line 184)**
    61→
    62→`weights[0, offset:offset+a_size]` — only the first batch element's weights are extracted. The function signature accepts batch input but silently discards all but the first. If someone passes batch_size > 1 expecting per-item adapters, they get duplicates. **Not a current bug (callers always pass batch=1) but a footgun.**
    63→
    64→**Weight head output dimensionality (lines 124–129)**
    65→
    66→`total_params = sum over modules of (num_layers * (rank * in_f + out_f * rank))`. For GQA models where `in_f != out_f` per module, this correctly allocates different sizes. **Correct.**
    67→
    68→But the head is a single `nn.Linear(latent_dim * num_latents, total_params)`. For typical configs (num_latents=32, latent_dim=256, num_layers=28, 2 modules, rank=8, hidden=4096):
    69→```
    70→input = 32 * 256 = 8,192
    71→output = 28 * 2 * (8 * 4096 + 4096 * 8) = 28 * 2 * 65536 = 3,670,016
    72→```
    73→That's a **3.67M-output linear layer with 8k inputs** = 30 billion parameters in the weight head alone. This is likely larger than the base model. **This is architecturally unsound** for any real use — the weight head dominates the parameter count and is essentially a memorization layer rather than a learned compression.
    74→
    75→### 1.3 QLoRA training (trainer.py)
    76→
    77→**Single-sample dataset (line 80)**
    78→
    79→`Dataset.from_list([{"messages": messages}])` — the entire training run operates on **one sample**. With `per_device_train_batch_size=1` and `gradient_accumulation_steps=4`, each epoch produces `floor(1/1) = 1` optimizer step per accumulation window, but with only 1 sample, the 4 accumulation steps see the same sample 4 times (if the dataloader wraps). Over 3 epochs: ~3 optimizer steps total with gradient from a single example. **This is memorization, not generalization.**
    80→
    81→**SFTTrainer `messages` field format (lines 79–80, 123–129)**
    82→
    83→The dataset column is `"messages"` (a list of role/content dicts). TRL's SFTTrainer (as of v0.8+) does support `"messages"` format and will apply the tokenizer's chat template automatically. **This is likely correct for recent TRL versions.** However, there is no explicit `dataset_text_field` or `formatting_func`, making it fragile across TRL version upgrades.
    84→
    85→**No learning rate warmup interaction check**
    86→
    87→`warmup_ratio=0.03` with 3 epochs × 1 step = 3 total steps → warmup = `ceil(0.03 * 3) = 1` step. The first step uses a reduced LR, leaving only 2 steps at full LR. For cosine scheduling over 3 steps, the LR curve is essentially noise. **The optimizer barely gets to learn.**
    88→
    89→### 1.4 TIES merge (merging.py)
    90→
    91→**Trim threshold via topk (lines 56–60)**
    92→
    93→```python
    94→k_keep = max(1, int(flat.numel() * density))
    95→threshold = torch.topk(flat, k_keep).values[-1]
    96→mask = stacked[i].abs() >= threshold
    97→```
    98→
    99→`topk` returns the k largest values. Taking `values[-1]` gives the k-th largest value. Then masking `>= threshold` keeps all values at or above that threshold. But **if multiple values are tied at the threshold, more than k values survive**. For the TIES algorithm, this is acceptable (density is approximate), but it means actual retained fraction can exceed `density`. Not a bug per se, but worth noting for reproducibility.
   100→
   101→**Sign election tie-breaking (lines 72–81)**
   102→
   103→When `elected_sign == 0` (true tie), the code falls through to include all surviving values via `keep_mask.float()`. This averages all positive and negative values together, which for a true tie tends toward zero. **This is a reasonable design choice** — the TIES paper doesn't specify tie-breaking behavior, and averaging toward zero is conservative.
   104→
   105→**Float precision loss (line 48)**
   106→
   107→`tensors = [sd[key].float() for sd in state_dicts]` — all tensors are cast to float32 for merging, then cast back on line 84: `.to(tensors[0].dtype)`. Since `tensors[0]` was already cast to float32 by line 48, the cast-back is a no-op. **The original dtype is lost.** If the input adapters were bfloat16, the merged output is always float32, which doubles the file size.
   108→
   109→### 1.5 DARE merge (merging.py)
   110→
   111→**Non-deterministic dropout (line 130)**
   112→
   113→`torch.rand_like(stacked[i]) >= drop_rate` — this uses the global RNG. The merge output is non-reproducible across runs. For an evolutionary system this may be acceptable (adds stochasticity), but **there is no way to set a seed for reproducible merges**, which makes debugging evolution failures difficult.
   114→
   115→### 1.6 Evolution sweep (swarm_evolution.py)
   116→
   117→**Active count computation (line 95)**
   118→
   119→```python
   120→"active": len(active) - pruned_count + merged_count
   121→```
   122→
   123→This counts archived adapters as "removed" from active, and adds 1 for each merge. But the merge *also creates a new adapter from the top-3*, which are themselves still active (not archived by the merge). So post-sweep, the actual active count is `len(active) - pruned_count + merged_count`, which **is correct** — the 1 merged adapter is new, and parent adapters remain active.
   124→
   125→However, **the pruning loop (lines 83–89) iterates over the same `active` list that was built before the merge**. If a merge result has `fitness_score < PRUNE_FITNESS_THRESHOLD` (unlikely for a merge of top-3, but possible), it won't be pruned until the next sweep because it's not in the `active` list. **Benign race but technically inconsistent.**
   126→
   127→**`_ties_merge_adapters` hardcodes `/tmp/` output (line 127)**
   128→
   129→`output_dir = f"/tmp/rune_merged/{merged_id}"` — not configurable, not cleaned up, and will fail on Windows. Should use the adapter base directory or tempdir.
   130→
   131→### 1.7 Adapter registry lineage (registry.py)
   132→
   133→**`get_lineage` follows only `parent_list[0]` (line 273)**
   134→
   135→For merged adapters, `parent_ids` is a JSON list of all parent adapter IDs. But lineage traversal takes only the first parent: `next_id = parent_list[0]`. This traces a single-parent ancestry chain rather than the full merge tree. **The lineage is incomplete for any adapter with multiple parents** (i.e., every merged adapter). This loses the graph structure that makes evolutionary lineage tracking useful.
   136→
   137→---
   138→
   139→## 2. Complexity Analysis
   140→
   141→### 2.1 Sakana HyperLoRA generation (`generate_adapter_from_sakana`)
   142→
   143→Let S = sequence length, L = total base model layers, L_sel = selected layer count, H = hidden dim, R = LoRA rank, M = number of target modules.
   144→
   145→| Step | Time | Space |
   146→|------|------|-------|
   147→| Load checkpoint | O(checkpoint_size) | O(checkpoint_size) |
   148→| Extract activations (full base forward + hidden states) | O(L · S² · H) | O(L · S · H) peak |
   149→| HyperLoRA perceiver forward | O(L_sel · S · latent_size) | O(L_sel · S · latent_size) |
   150→| combine_lora | O(L_sel · M · R · H) | O(L_sel · M · R · H) |
   151→| Save safetensors | O(output_size) | O(output_size) |
   152→
   153→**Bottleneck:** Activation extraction dominates — it requires a full forward pass through the base model with all hidden states materialized. For a 7B model with S=512: ~14GB peak memory for hidden states alone.
   154→
   155→**The base model is loaded *and discarded* every call** (lines 487–504 in `extract_activations`). If `generate_adapter_from_sakana` is called multiple times (e.g., per-subtask in the pipeline), this reloads the entire base model each time. The pipeline runner (`rune_runner.py`) already has the inference model loaded — but the activation extraction path doesn't reuse it.
   156→
   157→### 2.2 Custom hypernetwork forward (`DocToLoraHypernetwork.forward`)
   158→
   159→| Step | Time | Space |
   160→|------|------|-------|
   161→| Token embedding | O(B · S · D_lat) | O(B · S · D_lat) |
   162→| Cross-attention | O(B · N_lat · S · D_lat) | O(B · N_lat · D_lat) |
   163→| Self-attention (depth layers) | O(depth · B · N_lat² · D_lat) | O(B · N_lat · D_lat) |
   164→| Weight head linear | O(B · N_lat · D_lat · total_params) | O(B · total_params) |
   165→| Reshape loop | O(num_layers · M · (R·in + R·out)) | Negligible (views) |
   166→
   167→**The weight head linear is the bottleneck.** As computed above, for realistic configs it can have ~30B parameters. This makes the custom hypernetwork impractical for production use — it's orders of magnitude larger than the adapters it generates.
   168→
   169→### 2.3 QLoRA training
   170→
   171→With dataset size N=1, epochs E=3, sequence length S:
   172→
   173→| Step | Time | Space |
   174→|------|------|-------|
   175→| Model loading (NF4) | O(model_size) | O(model_size / 4) quantized |
   176→| Forward + backward per step | O(L · S² · H) | O(L · S · H) activations |
   177→| LoRA parameter update | O(L_sel · M · R · H) | O(L_sel · M · R · H) optimizer states |
   178→
   179→Total training: O(E · L · S² · H) = O(3 · L · S² · H). **Extremely fast** because N=1, but also **extremely underfitting**.
   180→
   181→### 2.4 TIES merge
   182→
   183→Let K = number of adapters, P = total parameters per adapter.
   184→
   185→| Step | Time | Space |
   186→|------|------|-------|
   187→| Stack tensors | O(K · P) | O(K · P) |
   188→| topk per tensor per adapter | O(K · P · log(density·P)) | O(density · P) |
   189→| Sign election + masking | O(K · P) | O(K · P) |
   190→| Weighted average | O(K · P) | O(P) output |
   191→
   192→**Overall: O(K · P · log P)** dominated by topk. For typical LoRA adapters (P ~ 1M params, K=3), this is fast (<1s).
   193→
   194→---
   195→
   196→## 3. Edge Cases and Robustness
   197→
   198→### 3.1 Data provenance gap — where does training data come from vs. should come from
   199→
   200→**Current state:** The QLoRA path loads trajectories from `~/.rune/trajectories/{session_id}.json`. These are written by `record_trajectory()` in `trajectory.py`. However, **`record_trajectory` is never called from `rune_runner.py`** (the main pipeline). The pipeline generates code but doesn't persist trajectories to disk in the format that `train_qlora` expects.
   201→
   202→This means:
   203→- The QLoRA training path (`train_qlora` → `load_trajectory`) expects trajectory files that **the main pipeline never writes**
   204→- The training pool manager (`swarm_workers.py`) calls `train_qlora(session_id=..., ...)` but there's no upstream code that maps pipeline session artifacts to trajectory JSON files
   205→- **The QLoRA path is effectively dead code** — it has the right structure but is disconnected from the data-producing pipeline
   206→
   207→**What should happen:** After each successful pipeline run, `rune_runner.py` should call `record_trajectory()` with the session's steps. The trajectory format needs to capture: task_description, generated_code per step, tests_passed per step, and the adapter_ids used.
   208→
   209→**The Sakana HyperLoRA path is properly connected** — it receives trajectory text directly from the template renderer (`render_trajectory`) and generates adapters in-line during the pipeline. No persistence gap.
   210→
   211→### 3.2 Trajectory text quality for hypernetwork
   212→
   213→The hypernetwork encodes *whatever text is rendered by the trajectory template* into adapter weights. The quality of the adapter depends entirely on the information density of this text. Currently:
   214→- Templates are Jinja2 (e.g., `code.j2`, `plan.j2`) rendering subtask descriptions, plans, and prior code
   215→- The text is truncated to `max_length=512` tokens for activation extraction
   216→- For complex subtasks with long code, **most of the trajectory content is truncated**, meaning the adapter sees only the first ~512 tokens of context
   217→
   218→This truncation is especially problematic for the code phase where the trajectory should encode the full subtask code + test results + dependency interfaces.
   219→
   220→### 3.3 Empty/short trajectory text
   221→
   222→If `text=""` is passed to `extract_activations`, the tokenizer produces a single BOS token. The resulting activations are essentially the model's idle state. The hypernetwork will produce adapter weights, but they'll be near-random (or biased toward whatever the checkpoint's latent initialization is). **No validation or warning for degenerate input.**
   223→
   224→### 3.4 Evolution with unevaluated adapters
   225→
   226→`evolution_sweep` queries `query_best_for_task` which filters on `fitness_score IS NOT NULL`. But newly created adapters (from hypernetwork or merge) have `fitness_score=None` until explicitly evaluated. If the evaluation pipeline is slow or broken, the evolution loop sees only old adapters. **There's no mechanism to trigger evaluation before a sweep** — these are decoupled.
   227→
   228→### 3.5 Adapter file path vs. registry record inconsistency
   229→
   230→In `_ties_merge_adapters`, the merged adapter is saved to `/tmp/rune_merged/{id}/adapter_model.safetensors` and registered with `file_path=f"{output_dir}/adapter_model.safetensors"`. But the `load_adapter_state_dict` function in `merging.py` takes a path to the `.safetensors` file directly. Meanwhile, `train_and_register` stores `file_path=output_dir` (the directory, not the file). **Inconsistent file_path semantics** — sometimes it's a directory, sometimes a file path.
   231→
   232→### 3.6 Concurrent SQLite access in swarm
   233→
   234→The training pool uses `ProcessPoolExecutor` with multiple workers. Each worker creates its own SQLAlchemy engine to the same SQLite database. While WAL mode is enabled, **SQLite has no row-level locking** — concurrent writes can still produce `SQLITE_BUSY` errors under contention. The registry operations don't retry on busy.
   235→
   236→### 3.7 Memory watchdog kills youngest agent indiscriminately
   237→
   238→`memory_watchdog` (swarm_workers.py:243–251) cancels `alive[-1]` when memory exceeds threshold. This assumes the list is ordered by creation time, but `agents` is just a `list[asyncio.Task]` — if tasks are removed/reordered, this may cancel the wrong agent. Also, cancelling a training task mid-write can corrupt adapter files.
   239→
   240→---
   241→
   242→## 4. Algorithmic Alternatives and Optimizations
   243→
   244→### A. Replace CRC32 trigram tokenizer with a proper tokenizer
   245→
   246→The custom `trajectory_to_tokens` (hypernetwork.py:286–302) uses CRC32 hash of character trigrams modulo vocab_size. This has high collision rates and no semantic structure.
   247→
   248→**Alternative:** Use the base model's tokenizer (or a fast BPE tokenizer like `tiktoken`) to produce token IDs, then map to the hypernetwork's vocabulary via a learned projection or direct embedding. This preserves token identity and eliminates hash collisions.
   249→
   250→**Trade-off:** Requires the tokenizer at inference time (already available in the pipeline). Adds a small dependency but dramatically improves input signal quality.
   251→
   252→### B. Cache base model for activation extraction
   253→
   254→Currently `extract_activations` loads the entire base model, runs one forward pass, then deletes it. In the pipeline, this can happen multiple times per session (once per subtask per retry).
   255→
   256→**Alternative:** Keep the base model loaded across the session and pass it to `extract_activations_with_model` directly. `rune_runner.py` already has the inference provider loaded — expose its underlying model for activation extraction.
   257→
   258→**Trade-off:** Increases steady-state memory by ~model_size, but eliminates repeated load/unload cycles that dominate latency in the hypernetwork path.
   259→
   260→### C. Restructure DocToLoraHypernetwork weight head
   261→
   262→The single linear layer from `latent_dim * num_latents → total_params` creates an astronomically large parameter matrix.
   263→
   264→**Alternative:** Use a **factored head** — project latents to a per-layer, per-module representation, then use small per-module linear layers to produce A and B matrices:
   265→```
   266→latents → per_layer_repr (Linear: N_lat*D_lat → num_layers * layer_repr_dim)
   267→per_layer_repr[i] → per_module_repr (Linear: layer_repr_dim → module_repr_dim)
   268→per_module_repr → lora_A (Linear: module_repr_dim → rank * in_f)
   269→per_module_repr → lora_B (Linear: module_repr_dim → out_f * rank)
   270→```
   271→
   272→This reduces the weight head from O(N_lat · D_lat · num_layers · M · R · H) parameters to O(N_lat · D_lat · num_layers · D_repr + num_layers · M · D_repr · R · max(in_f, out_f)), which is orders of magnitude smaller.
   273→
   274→**Trade-off:** Adds architectural complexity but makes the hypernetwork practically trainable and reduces overfitting risk.
   275→
   276→### D. Multi-sample QLoRA training
   277→
   278→Instead of training on a single successful trajectory step:
   279→1. Include **all successful steps** from the trajectory (not just the last one)
   280→2. Include steps from **related trajectories** (same task_type, high fitness)
   281→3. Use a **curriculum** — order by increasing difficulty (early attempts → final solution)
   282→
   283→**Trade-off:** Requires trajectory storage and retrieval infrastructure (which exists but is disconnected — see Section 3.1). Dramatically improves training signal.
   284→
   285→### E. Lineage as DAG instead of linked list
   286→
   287→Replace `get_lineage`'s single-parent traversal with a proper DAG traversal:
   288→```python
   289→def get_lineage_dag(self, adapter_id: str) -> dict[str, list[str]]:
   290→    """Return full parent DAG as adjacency list."""
   291→    dag = {}
   292→    queue = [adapter_id]
   293→    while queue:
   294→        current = queue.pop(0)
   295→        if current in dag:
   296→            continue
   297→        record = self.retrieve_by_id(current)
   298→        parents = json.loads(record.parent_ids) if record.parent_ids else []
   299→        dag[current] = parents
   300→        queue.extend(p for p in parents if p not in dag)
   301→    return dag
   302→```
   303→
   304→**Trade-off:** More queries but gives the full evolutionary tree. Enables proper lineage visualization and ancestor-aware selection.
   305→
   306→### F. Reproducible DARE merging
   307→
   308→Add a `seed` parameter to `dare_merge`:
   309→```python
   310→def dare_merge(state_dicts, drop_rate=0.1, seed=None):
   311→    if seed is not None:
   312→        torch.manual_seed(seed)
   313→    ...
   314→```
   315→
   316→**Trade-off:** Minimal code change, enables reproducible debugging of evolution failures.
   317→
   318→### G. Preserve original dtype through merging
   319→
   320→Replace the float32 conversion with tracking original dtypes:
   321→```python
   322→original_dtype = state_dicts[0][key].dtype  # save before conversion
   323→# ... merge in float32 ...
   324→merged[key] = result.to(original_dtype)  # restore
   325→```
   326→
   327→**Trade-off:** None — this is strictly better.
   328→
   329→---
   330→
   331→## 5. Focused Improvement Checklist
   332→
   333→### Critical (correctness/functionality)
   334→
   335→1. **Connect trajectory recording to pipeline execution.** `rune_runner.py` must call `record_trajectory()` after each session. Without this, the QLoRA path has no training data — it's architecturally complete but operationally dead. This is the single highest-impact gap.
   336→
   337→2. **Fix DocToLoraHypernetwork weight head scaling.** The single-linear-layer head produces ~30B parameters for realistic configs, making the custom hypernetwork untrained and unusable. Either factorize the head (option C above) or remove the custom hypernetwork in favor of Sakana-only.
   338→
   339→3. **Replace CRC32 trigram tokenizer.** The hash-based tokenizer in `trajectory_to_tokens` has high collision rates that degrade hypernetwork input quality. Use a proper tokenizer to preserve token identity.
   340→
   341→### Important (robustness/efficiency)
   342→
   343→4. **Cache base model during activation extraction.** Avoid reloading the base model for every `generate_adapter_from_sakana` call. Reuse the inference provider's loaded model.
   344→
   345→5. **Fix dtype preservation in TIES/DARE merge** — merged adapters are always float32 regardless of input dtype, doubling file size for bfloat16 adapters.
   346→
   347→6. **Add seed parameter to DARE merge** for reproducible evolution debugging.
   348→
   349→7. **Fix `get_lineage` to traverse full parent DAG** instead of single-parent chain, so evolutionary lineage tracking actually works for merged adapters.
   350→
   351→### Nice-to-have (hardening)
   352→
   353→8. **Validate adapter file_path semantics** — standardize whether `file_path` in AdapterRecord points to the directory or the `.safetensors` file.
   354→
   355→9. **Add SQLite busy-retry logic** in the adapter registry for concurrent swarm access.
   356→
   357→10. **Guard against empty/degenerate trajectory text** in both hypernetwork paths — warn or skip when text is empty or below a minimum token count.
   358→
```

Perfect! Now I have a comprehensive understanding of the Rune training pipeline. Let me compile a detailed report:

## Comprehensive Rune Training Pipeline Analysis

I've thoroughly explored the Rune training pipeline code. Here's a detailed breakdown of the key files and their implementations:

---

### 1. **`libs/model-training/src/model_training/trainer.py`** — QLoRA Training Orchestrator

**Key Functions:**

- **`train_qlora(session_id, adapter_id, output_dir, *, base_model_id=None, task_type="code-gen", rank=64, alpha=128, epochs=3, learning_rate=2e-4) -> str`**
  - Trains a QLoRA adapter from a recorded coding trajectory
  - Full pipeline: load trajectory → format as SFT messages → build dataset → NF4 quantization → SFTTrainer
  - **Key guards**: FileNotFoundError if trajectory missing; ValueError if not successful or has no SFT messages
  - **Deferred imports**: torch, datasets, transformers, trl all imported in function body (INFRA-05 pattern)
  - Uses BitsAndBytesConfig with NF4 quantization and bfloat16 compute dtype to prevent NaN loss
  - Target modules: ["q_proj", "v_proj"]
  - Saves via `trainer.save_model()` (PEFT-aware: safetensors + adapter_config.json)

- **`train_and_register(session_id, adapter_id, *, base_model_id=None, ..., database_url=None) -> str`**
  - Combines train_qlora() with AdapterRegistry.store()
  - Computes SHA-256 hash and file size of adapter_model.safetensors
  - Creates AdapterRecord with timestamp, source="qlora", session_id linkage
  - Resolves RUNE_ADAPTER_DIR (~/.rune/adapters/{adapter_id}) and RUNE_DATABASE_URL env vars inside function

**Test Coverage** (test_trainer.py):
- CPU-only importability check
- FileNotFoundError for missing trajectory
- ValueError for non-successful (outcome='exhausted') trajectories
- train_and_register creates adapter dir and stores registry record (xfail due to GPU mocking fragility)

---

### 2. **`libs/model-training/src/model_training/trajectory.py`** — Trajectory Handling

**Key Functions:**

- **`_get_trajectory_dir() -> Path`**
  - Reads RUNE_TRAJECTORY_DIR env var inside function body (not module-level) for monkeypatch testability
  - Default: `~/.rune/trajectories`

- **`record_trajectory(session_id, steps, outcome=None, *, task_description="", task_type="", adapter_ids=None) -> dict`**
  - Persists trajectory JSON to disk
  - Structure: `{ session_id, task_description, task_type, adapter_ids, outcome, timestamp, steps }`
  - Creates directory if missing
  - Returns `{ session_id, file_path }`

- **`load_trajectory(trajectory_id) -> dict`**
  - Loads trajectory by session ID
  - Raises FileNotFoundError naturally if file missing
  - **No validation** — returns raw dict

- **`format_for_sft(trajectory) -> list[dict[str, str]]`**
  - Converts successful trajectories to SFT chat format
  - **Guard**: returns `[]` if `outcome != "success"` or no step has `tests_passed=True`
  - Extracts last step with `tests_passed=True`
  - Returns: `[{role: "system", content: SYSTEM_PROMPT}, {role: "user", content: task_description}, {role: "assistant", content: generated_code}]`
  - SYSTEM_PROMPT = "You are a Python code generator. Output only code, no explanation."

**Test Coverage** (test_trajectory.py):
- JSON writing, directory creation, metadata inclusion
- Round-trip load/record
- FileNotFoundError for missing files
- SFT format: success/exhausted/no-passing-step cases
- 6 comprehensive tests

---

### 3. **`libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_train.py`** — KL-Divergence Context Distillation

**Configuration (D2LTrainConfig — Pydantic model):**
- Validators: lr > 0, alpha ∈ [0, 1], temperature > 0, num_steps > 0
- Key fields: base_model_name, sakana_checkpoint_path, num_steps, lr, alpha, temperature, checkpoint_every, full_checkpoint_every, dry_run, smoke_test, dataset_path, grad_clip, warmup_steps, lora_r, max_length

**Key Functions:**

- **`_compute_kl_ce_loss(student_logits, teacher_logits, answer_start, config) -> (loss, metrics)`**
  - Slices both logits to `[:, answer_start:, :]` (answer-span masking)
  - KL divergence: temperature-scaled with log_softmax on student, softmax on teacher
  - CE: student preds vs teacher hard labels (argmax)
  - Blended: `alpha * kl + (1 - alpha) * ce`
  - Returns: (scalar loss tensor, {kl_loss, ce_loss, total_loss} as floats)
  - **Key insight**: answer_start masking allows context/answer separation

- **`_training_step(record, base_model, tokenizer, hypernet, hc, config) -> (loss, metrics)`**
  - **Two-pass separation**:
    1. Pass 1: extract activations from activation_text only (no answer tokens)
    2. Pass 2: teacher (no_grad) and student (with LoRA) on teacher_text
  - Computes answer_start from len(tokenized activation_text)
  - Uses `apply_functional_lora()` context manager to inject LoRA weights
  - Preserves autograd graph through LoRA tensors
  - **Critical**: hypernet.generate_weights() call is OUTSIDE torch.no_grad()

- **`_save_checkpoint(step, hypernet, optimizer, scheduler, config, hc, best_loss, full=False) -> Path`**
  - Lightweight: hypernet_state_dict, config_json, step, attention_layer_indices, best_loss
  - Full (full=True): + optimizer_state_dict, scheduler_state_dict, rng_state, cuda_rng_state
  - Saves to `{checkpoint_dir}/ckpt-{step}.pt`

- **`train_d2l_qwen3(config) -> dict`**
  - **Three modes**:
    1. dry_run: Validate shapes with single forward pass, exit
    2. smoke_test: Run min(num_steps, 5), assert finite decreasing loss
    3. full: Full training loop with checkpointing and MLflow
  - Guard: _require_probe_cache() ensures probe cache exists (except smoke_test)
  - Setup:
    - Loads tokenizer and base model (eval mode)
    - Builds hypernet config from aggregator config in checkpoint
    - transfer_aggregator_weights() freezes aggregator, leaves head trainable
  - Optimizer: AdamW scoped to trainable params only
  - Scheduler: Linear warmup → CosineAnnealingLR
  - Data: generate_needle_dataset(n=20) or load_jsonl(dataset_path)
  - MLflow logging: params and per-step metrics
  - Tiered checkpointing: lightweight every N steps, full every M steps
  - Smoke test assertions: finite losses, decreasing trend, non-None gradients

**Test Coverage** (test_d2l_train.py):
- D2LTrainConfig Pydantic validation (lr, alpha, temperature, num_steps)
- _compute_kl_ce_loss: KL~0 when equal, KL>0 when different, CE valid, alpha blending, answer_start masking
- Checkpoint save/load roundtrip, required keys
- Frozen aggregator has None grad, trainable head has gradients
- Two-pass separation shapes
- 5-step loss finite smoke test

---

### 4. **`libs/model-training/src/model_training/hypernetwork.py`** — DocToLoraHypernetwork

**Architecture (nn.Module subclass, built lazily via _build_hypernetwork_class()):**
- Token embedding: lightweight (not 7B model's) — input_dim → latent_dim
- Learnable latents: (num_latents=32, latent_dim=256) as nn.Parameter
- Cross-attention: latents attend over token embeddings
- Self-attention stack: process latents
- Weight head: Linear(num_latents * latent_dim → total_weight_params)

**Key Functions:**

- **`forward(token_ids: (batch, seq_len)) -> dict[str, Tensor]`**
  - Embeds tokens → cross-attention → self-attention → flatten → project
  - Returns PEFT state_dict: `base_model.model.model.layers.{i}.self_attn.{module}.lora_{A|B}.weight`
  - Extracts first batch element (one adapter per trajectory)
  - **Output format**: lora_A (rank, in_features), lora_B (out_features, rank)

- **`load_pretrained(checkpoint_path, device="cpu", **kwargs) -> DocToLoraHypernetwork`**
  - Loads from .pt checkpoint with constructor args
  - Sets default input_dim if missing
  - Returns model in eval mode

- **`trajectory_to_tokens(trajectory_text, vocab_size=32000, max_length=2048) -> Tensor`**
  - Hash-based tokenization: character trigrams → crc32 % vocab_size
  - Pads or truncates to max_length
  - Returns (1, max_length) token tensor

- **`generate_adapter(hypernetwork, trajectory_text, output_dir, base_model_id, ...) -> str`**
  - End-to-end: trajectory_to_tokens → forward → save_hypernetwork_adapter

- **`save_hypernetwork_adapter(weights, output_dir, base_model_id, rank=8, target_modules=None)`**
  - Writes adapter_model.safetensors (via safetensors.torch.save_file)
  - Writes adapter_config.json with PEFT fields:
    - peft_type: "LORA", r: rank, lora_alpha: rank*2, target_modules, lora_dropout: 0.0, bias: "none"
    - task_type: "CAUSAL_LM", modules_to_save: None (vLLM rejects this)
  - **Key guard**: Raises FileExistsError if adapter already exists

**Lazy Proxy Pattern:**
- `_LazyHypernetworkProxy` builds real nn.Module class on first instantiation
- Keeps module importable without torch at module level

**Test Coverage** (test_hypernetwork.py):
- CPU-only importability without GPU
- Latents shape (32, 256)
- forward() PEFT key naming convention
- forward() lora_A/B shapes (rank, hidden) / (hidden, rank)
- save_hypernetwork_adapter writes files and config fields
- forward() completes without base model loading

---

### 5. **`libs/model-training/src/model_training/sakana_d2l.py`** — Sakana Doc-to-LoRA Integration

**Key Functions:**

- **`_patch_flash_attention()`**
  - Injects flash_attn stub module to bypass import checks
  - Patches Idefics2Perceiver, Idefics2PerceiverResampler, Idefics2PerceiverAttention to use eager attention
  - Removes flash-specific kwargs (cu_seq_lens_q, cu_seq_lens_k, max_length_q, max_length_k)
  - Forces eager attn implementation in all configs

- **`download_checkpoint(variant="gemma_demo") -> Path`**
  - HuggingFace variants: gemma_demo, gemma_2b_d2l, mistral_7b_d2l, qwen_4b_d2l
  - Caches to `~/.cache/rune/sakana_d2l/{variant}/pytorch_model.bin`
  - Uses huggingface_hub.hf_hub_download

- **`load_sakana_checkpoint(checkpoint_path=None, variant="gemma_demo", device="cpu") -> (hypernet, hc)`**
  - Downloads checkpoint if path is None
  - Patches flash attention before loading
  - Loads with torch.load(..., weights_only=False)
  - Extracts HypernetConfig and creates HyperLoRA instance
  - Resolves dtype via resolve_model_dtype() based on param count
  - Returns hypernet in eval mode

- **`transfer_aggregator_weights(hypernet, checkpoint_path) -> hypernet`**
  - Loads only aggregator.* weights from checkpoint
  - Freezes aggregator (requires_grad=False)
  - Leaves head at PyTorch default init for Phase 29 training
  - Validates transfer integrity with _assert_transfer_integrity()
  - Returns hypernet (mutated in-place, returned for chaining)

- **`_assert_transfer_integrity(hypernet, loaded)`**
  - Checks: no unexpected keys, missing keys are head.* only, no aggregator.* missing
  - Raises AssertionError with diagnostic messages if mismatch detected

- **`get_aggregator_config(checkpoint_path) -> aggregator_config`**
  - Extracts aggregator_config from checkpoint's HypernetConfig
  - Used by d2l_config.build_qwen3_hypernet_config()
  - Raises ValueError if aggregator_config is None (old checkpoint)

- **`extract_activations(text, base_model_name, layer_indices, device="cpu", max_length=512) -> (features, attn_mask)`**
  - Backward-compatible wrapper: loads model, delegates to extract_activations_with_model(), cleans up
  - Resolves dtype via resolve_model_dtype() with estimated param count overhead

- **`generate_adapter_from_sakana(text, output_dir, checkpoint_path=None, variant="gemma_demo", base_model_name=None, device="cpu", max_length=512, scaling_factor=0.16) -> str`**
  - **Main entry point**: text → activations → HyperLoRA → PEFT adapter
  - Loads Sakana perceiver
  - Extracts per-layer activations from base model
  - Runs hypernet.generate_weights() with no_grad
  - Calls combine_lora() to concatenate bias as extra rank dimensions
  - Saves via _save_sakana_adapter()
  - Frees VRAM after saving

- **`_save_sakana_adapter(lora_dict, output_dir, base_model_name, hc, scaling_factor=0.16)`**
  - Converts Sakana format → PEFT flat state_dict
  - Sakana format: lora_dict[module_name]["A"] (batch, num_layers, rank, in)
  - PEFT format: `base_model.model.model.layers.{layer_idx}.{prefix}.{module}.lora_{A|B}.weight`
  - Takes first batch element, transposes B to (out_features, rank)
  - **Rank combination**: After combine_lora(), rank may be 2*base_rank (bias concatenated)
  - **Alpha scaling**: `peft_alpha = checkpoint_alpha * actual_rank * scaling_factor`
    - Compensation for Sakana's direct alpha vs PEFT's alpha/r division
  - Writes adapter_config.json with actual_rank and computed alpha
  - Guards: Raises FileExistsError if adapter already exists

**Key Guard**: _require_probe_cache() in d2l_train.py ensures probe cache exists before training with real dataset

---

### 6. **Supporting Utilities**

**`libs/model-training/src/model_training/peft_utils.py`:**
- `build_qlora_config(rank, alpha, target_modules, dropout=0.1)` — Returns LoraConfig with CAUSAL_LM task
- `apply_lora_adapter(model, config)` — Wraps model with get_peft_model()
- `merge_adapter(model)` — Not implemented (NotImplementedError)

**`libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_lora.py`:**
- `apply_functional_lora(model, lora_dict, hc)` — Context manager for functional LoRA injection
  - Patches modules' forward methods with closures over A/B tensors
  - Preserves autograd graph through LoRA weights
  - Formula: `base_linear(x) + linear(linear(x, A), B.t()) * scale`
  - Restoration via `module.__dict__.pop("forward", None)` on exit

**`libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_config.py`:**
- `get_d2l_qwen3_config()` — Returns Qwen3-Coder-Next architecture dims (hidden_size=2048, num_layers=48, full_attention indices=[3,7,11,...,47])
- `build_qwen3_hypernet_config(lora_r=8, target_modules=None, aggregator_config=None)` — Builds HypernetConfig with probe cache integration or hidden_size placeholders

**`libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_probe.py`:**
- `probe_model(model)` — Discovers standard attention layers with all 4 projections (q/k/v/o)
- `save_probe_cache(model_name, probe_result)` — Persists to JSON with metadata
- `load_probe_cache(model_name)` — Returns cached probe or None (never raises)
- `extract_activations_with_model(text, model, tokenizer, layer_indices, max_length)` — Extracts per-layer hidden states

---

### Summary of Existing Validation & Guards

| Module | Function | Guard | Behavior |
|--------|----------|-------|----------|
| trainer.py | train_qlora | trajectory missing | FileNotFoundError |
| trainer.py | train_qlora | not successful or no SFT | ValueError |
| trajectory.py | format_for_sft | outcome != "success" | return [] |
| trajectory.py | format_for_sft | no tests_passed step | return [] |
| d2l_train.py | D2LTrainConfig | lr <= 0 | Pydantic ValidationError |
| d2l_train.py | D2LTrainConfig | alpha ∉ [0,1] | Pydantic ValidationError |
| d2l_train.py | D2LTrainConfig | temperature <= 0 | Pydantic ValidationError |
| d2l_train.py | D2LTrainConfig | num_steps <= 0 | Pydantic ValidationError |
| d2l_train.py | train_d2l_qwen3 | probe cache missing | RuntimeError (not smoke_test) |
| d2l_lora.py | apply_functional_lora | shape mismatch | RuntimeError |
| sakana_d2l.py | transfer_aggregator_weights | integrity fail | AssertionError |
| sakana_d2l.py | _save_sakana_adapter | adapter exists | FileExistsError |
| hypernetwork.py | save_hypernetwork_adapter | adapter exists | FileExistsError |

---

### Key Architectural Insights

1. **INFRA-05 Pattern**: All GPU imports (torch, transformers, peft, safetensors) deferred inside function bodies for CPU-only importability in CI
2. **Two-Pass Training**: activation_text (context only) separate from teacher_text (full context+answer) allows targeted loss computation
3. **Functional LoRA**: Context manager patches module.forward with closures over A/B tensors, preserving autograd graph unlike PEFT's get_peft_model
4. **Aggregator Freezing**: Sakana's pretrained perceiver frozen, only head retrained per target model
5. **Answer-Span Masking**: _compute_kl_ce_loss slices to [answer_start:] to exclude context tokens from loss
6. **Probe Cache**: Single source of truth for layer indices and projection dimensions across pipeline
7. **Hash-Based Tokenization**: hypernetwork's own lightweight embedding using trigram CRC32 hashing (not 7B model's vocab)

All files follow strict type hints, Google-style docstrings, and comprehensive validation patterns.

Perfect! Now I have a comprehensive understanding of the code. Let me create a detailed summary document:

## Summary: Rune Merging, Evolution, and Registry Code Analysis

Based on my exploration, here's a comprehensive breakdown of the key modules:

---

### 1. **`libs/model-training/src/model_training/merging.py`**

**Purpose:** Implements TIES and DARE merge strategies for combining multiple LoRA adapters.

**Key Functions:**

- **`ties_merge(state_dicts: list[dict[str, Any]], density: float = 0.5) -> dict[str, Any]`**
  - **Algorithm:** Trim-Elect-Sign-Disjoint merge
    1. Trim: For each parameter, keeps top `density` fraction of values by absolute magnitude
    2. Elect: Majority sign vote using only non-trimmed values
    3. Disjoint: Averages only values matching elected sign
    4. Tie-breaking: Where sign votes tie (count==0), averages all survivors
  - **Edge Cases:**
    - Empty `state_dicts` → raises `ValueError`
    - `density` outside [0.0, 1.0] → raises `ValueError`
    - Empty tensors → skipped gracefully
    - Dtype handling: Converts to `float()` for computation, restores `tensors[0].dtype` in output
  - **Determinism:** Fully deterministic (no randomness)

- **`dare_merge(state_dicts: list[dict[str, Any]], drop_rate: float = 0.1) -> dict[str, Any]`**
  - **Algorithm:** Drop-And-REscale merge
    1. For each state dict: randomly drop `drop_rate` fraction, rescale survivors by `1.0 / (1.0 - drop_rate)`
    2. Average across all state dicts
  - **Edge Cases:**
    - Empty `state_dicts` → raises `ValueError`
    - `drop_rate` outside [0.0, 1.0) → raises `ValueError` (note: must be < 1.0, not <= 1.0)
    - `drop_rate == 0.0` → equivalent to simple mean
  - **Determinism:** Non-deterministic (uses `torch.rand_like()`)
  - **Special:** Uses `scale = 1.0 / (1.0 - drop_rate)` for unbiased averaging

- **`load_adapter_state_dict(adapter_path: str | Path) -> dict[str, Any]`**
  - Defers `safetensors.torch.load_file()` inside function body
  - Loads to CPU by default (`device="cpu"`)

**Test Coverage** (`libs/model-training/tests/test_merging.py`):
- Shape preservation (both merge methods)
- DARE: zero drop-rate equals mean
- TIES/DARE: empty input validation
- TIES: density bounds [0.0, 1.0]
- DARE: drop_rate bounds [0.0, 1.0)
- TIES: sign tie handling (averages survivors)
- Safetensors loading via mock

---

### 2. **`scripts/swarm_evolution.py`**

**Purpose:** Evolution worker managing adapter fitness, merging, and pruning.

**Key Functions:**

- **`evaluate_adapter(adapter_id: str, pass_rate: float, diversity_score: float, registry: "AdapterRegistry") -> float`**
  - Calls `evaluate_fitness()` from `evaluation.metrics`
  - Updates registry with `pass_rate` and `fitness_score`
  - Returns computed fitness

- **`evolution_sweep(registry: "AdapterRegistry") -> dict[str, Any]`**
  - **Sweep Logic:**
    1. For each task type:
       - Filter active adapters (not archived)
       - If `len(active) >= MERGE_MIN_ADAPTERS` (=5):
         - Query top-3 best adapters
         - Filter out adapters with `fitness_score == None`
         - If >= 2 remain, TIES-merge them
       - Prune adapters with `fitness_score < PRUNE_FITNESS_THRESHOLD` (=0.3)
  - **Edge Cases:**
    - Filters NULL fitness scores from merge candidates
    - Gracefully logs merge failures
  - **Return:** Dict with per-task-type: `{"merged": count, "pruned": count, "active": count}`

- **`_ties_merge_adapters(adapter_ids: list[str], task_type: str, registry: "AdapterRegistry") -> str`**
  - **Hardcoded /tmp:** `output_dir = f"/tmp/rune_merged/{merged_id}"`
  - **Workflow:**
    1. Loads state dicts from registry via `retrieve_by_id()`
    2. TIES-merges with default `density=0.5`
    3. Creates `AdapterRecord` with:
       - `generation = max(parent_generations) + 1`
       - `source = "evolution"`
       - `session_id = "merge"`
       - `parent_ids = json.dumps(adapter_ids)` (lineage tracking)
       - `file_hash = ""`, `created_at = ""`
    4. Saves to `/tmp/rune_merged/{merged_id}/adapter_model.safetensors`
    5. Registers in registry

- **`evolution_worker(registry: "AdapterRegistry", interval_seconds: int = 7200, shutdown_event: asyncio.Event | None = None) -> None`**
  - Async loop calling `evolution_sweep()` every `interval_seconds` (default 2 hours)

**Test Coverage** (`tests/test_swarm_evolution.py`):
- Adapter evaluation updates fitness
- Pruning of low-fitness adapters
- Merge skipped when < 5 adapters
- Merge triggered with >= 5 adapters
- Exclude NULL fitness from merge candidates
- No pruning above threshold

**Thresholds:**
```python
MERGE_MIN_ADAPTERS = 5
MERGE_TOP_K = 3
PRUNE_FITNESS_THRESHOLD = 0.3
```

---

### 3. **`libs/adapter-registry/src/adapter_registry/registry.py`**

**Purpose:** SQLite-backed CRUD registry for adapter metadata.

**Key Methods:**

- **Constructor:** `__init__(engine: Engine) -> None`
  - Registers WAL mode hook via `set_wal_mode(engine)`
  - Creates tables idempotently via `SQLModel.metadata.create_all(engine)`

- **Write Operations:**
  - `store(record: AdapterRecord)` → raises `AdapterAlreadyExistsError` on duplicate ID
  - `archive(adapter_id: str)` → soft-delete by setting `is_archived=True`
  - `update_fitness(adapter_id: str, pass_rate: float, fitness_score: float)` → updates metrics

- **Read Operations:**
  - `retrieve_by_id(adapter_id: str)` → single record or raises `AdapterNotFoundError`
  - `query_by_task_type(task_type: str)` → all records matching task type
  - `list_all()` → all non-archived records
  - `query_best_for_task(task_type: str, top_k: int = 3)` → top-k by fitness DESC, excluding archived & NULL fitness
  - `is_task_solved(task_hash: str, threshold: float = 0.95)` → checks if any adapter for hash meets threshold
  - `get_lineage(adapter_id: str) -> list[str]` → walks `parent_ids` chain back to root (with cycle detection)
  - `query_unevaluated(task_type: str | None = None)` → adapters with `pass_rate IS NULL`
  - `get_task_types()` → sorted distinct task types

- **`file_path` Semantics:**
  - Stored in `AdapterRecord.file_path: str`
  - Used by `_ties_merge_adapters()` to load state dicts via `load_adapter_state_dict(a.file_path)`
  - No validation of path existence; registry is metadata-only
  - Evolution sets it to `/tmp/rune_merged/{merged_id}/adapter_model.safetensors`

- **`parent_ids` Semantics:**
  - Stored as JSON string: `parent_ids: str | None = Field(default=None)`
  - Parsed in `get_lineage()` via `json.loads(record.parent_ids)` → `list[str]`
  - Set by `_ties_merge_adapters()` to track parents: `parent_ids=json.dumps(adapter_ids)`

**Test Coverage** (`libs/adapter-registry/tests/test_registry.py`):
- Store, retrieve, query, archive, update_fitness
- Lineage walking (single + chain)
- is_task_solved filtering
- WAL mode verification
- Concurrent writes (5 threads, no deadlock)

---

### 4. **`scripts/swarm_workers.py`**

**Purpose:** Training pool manager and memory watchdog for GPU time-sharing.

**Key Components:**

- **`TrainingRequest` Dataclass:**
  ```python
  session_id: str
  task_type: str
  ```

- **`_drain_queue(queue: asyncio.Queue[TrainingRequest], max_batch: int, timeout: float = 1.0) -> list[TrainingRequest]`**
  - Blocks for first item with `timeout`
  - Grabs remaining items non-blocking up to `max_batch`
  - Returns empty list if timeout

- **`_train_in_subprocess(session_id: str, task_type: str, adapter_dir: str, db_url: str) -> dict[str, Any]`**
  - Generates UUID for adapter_id inside function (picklable)
  - Calls `train_qlora()` with INFRA-05 pattern (deferred import)
  - Gracefully handles missing `model_training.trainer` (logs warning)
  - Returns dict: `{"adapter_id", "session_id", "task_type", "adapter_dir"}`

- **`training_pool_manager(training_queue: asyncio.Queue[TrainingRequest], budget: "HardwareBudget", ...)`**
  - Env vars (with defaults):
    - `RUNE_DB_URL` → `"sqlite:///rune_swarm.db"`
    - `RUNE_ADAPTER_DIR` → `"/tmp/rune_adapters"` (hardcoded /tmp)
    - `RUNE_VLLM_URL` → `"http://localhost:8100/v1"`
  - Uses `ProcessPoolExecutor` with `max_workers = budget.training_slots`
  - In single-GPU mode: sleep vLLM before training, wake after
  - Drains batch from queue and submits to executor

- **`memory_watchdog(budget: "HardwareBudget", agents: list[asyncio.Task[Any]], check_interval: float = 30.0, threshold: float = 0.85, shutdown_event: asyncio.Event | None = None)`**
  - Monitors system memory via `psutil.virtual_memory()`
  - If `usage > 0.85` and agents exist: cancels youngest agent and removes from list
  - Takes snapshot of `agents` list before iteration (mutation-safe)

**Hardcoded /tmp:**
```python
adapter_base_dir or os.environ.get("RUNE_ADAPTER_DIR", "/tmp/rune_adapters")
```

**Test Coverage** (`tests/test_swarm_workers.py`):
- Queue draining (batch collection, empty handling)
- Subprocess training returns result dict
- Memory watchdog cancels on high pressure (90%)
- Memory watchdog does nothing when below threshold (50%)

---

### 5. **Trajectory Recording Flow**

**Module:** `libs/model-training/src/model_training/trajectory.py`

**Functions:**

- **`record_trajectory(session_id: str, steps: list[dict[str, Any]], outcome: Optional[str] = None, *, task_description: str = "", task_type: str = "", adapter_ids: list[str] | None = None) -> dict[str, Any]`**
  - Respects `RUNE_TRAJECTORY_DIR` env var (defaults to `~/.rune/trajectories`)
  - Writes JSON file: `{session_id}.json`
  - Returns: `{"session_id", "file_path"}`
  - **Not called in `rune_runner.py`**
  - **Called in `save_trajectory_node()` from `services/rune-agent/src/rune_agent/nodes.py`**

- **`load_trajectory(trajectory_id: str) -> dict[str, Any]`**
  - Loads from disk
  - Raises `FileNotFoundError` if missing

- **`format_for_sft(trajectory: dict[str, Any]) -> list[dict[str, str]]`**
  - Extracts SFT chat format: `[system, user, assistant]`
  - Only for `outcome == "success"`
  - Finds last step with `tests_passed == True`

**Call Sites:**
- `services/rune-agent/src/rune_agent/nodes.py:306` — `save_trajectory_node()`
- `scripts/e2e_smoke.py` — end-to-end smoke test

**Test Coverage** (`libs/model-training/tests/test_trajectory.py`):
- JSON writing and round-trip loading
- Directory creation
- Task metadata inclusion
- SFT format extraction
- Missing file handling

**GPU/Optional Dependencies (ignore_missing_imports):**
- PyTorch ecosystem: `torch.*`, `transformers.*`, `peft.*`
- Inference: `bitsandbytes.*`, `safetensors.*`, `trl.*`, `openai.*`
- Specialized: `flash_attn.*`, `ctx_to_lora.*`
- Core infrastructure: `fastapi`, `pydantic`, `sqlalchemy`, `sqlmodel`
- Data: `datasets.*`, `langchain_*`, `langgraph.*`

---

## Pytest Configuration (pyproject.toml)

```toml
[tool.pytest.ini_options]
testpaths = ["services", "libs", "tests"]
addopts = "-n auto --dist loadgroup --import-mode=importlib"
asyncio_mode = "auto"
pythonpath = [
    "services/api-service/src",
    "services/evolution-svc/src",
    "services/rune-agent/src",
    "services/training-svc/src",
    "libs/adapter-registry/src",
    "libs/evaluation/src",
    "libs/events-py/src",
    "libs/inference/src",
    "libs/model-training/src",
    "libs/shared/src",
]
```

**Filter Warnings:** Suppresses DeprecationWarning (SwigPy*), ResourceWarning (unclosed db), pytest warnings, coverage warnings

**Coverage Exclusions:**
- All test files
- Virtual environments
- Pragma no cover, __repr__, assertions, NotImplementedError, TYPE_CHECKING blocks

---

## Key Test Patterns Observed

1. **GPU-optional testing:** Tests use `@requires_torch` markers and sys.modules injection (fake torch, safetensors) to run on CPU-only CI
2. **Hypernetwork tests:** Verify latent parameter shapes (32, 256), PEFT key naming conventions, adapter config fields
3. **Merging tests:** TIES/DARE validation, density/drop_rate bounds, sign-tie fallback, shape preservation
4. **Training tests:** QLoRA config building, trajectory validation, checkpoint saving
5. **Adapter registry:** Full CRUD with AdapterAlreadyExistsError and AdapterNotFoundError handling
6. **Swarm tests:** Queue drainage, subprocess training, memory watchdog, evolution sweep, checkpoint recovery
7. **Data pipeline:** Trajectory formatting, augmentation, task_id splitting with zero leakage

---

## Summary

- **Total Tests:** 352 collected across 48 test files
- **Covered Modules:** Training (hypernetwork, merging, D2L pipeline), adapter registry, inference, evaluation, shared utilities, services
- **CI Strategy:** Conditional path-based triggering, parallel test execution (12 workers via xdist), coverage reporting
- **Type Safety:** Strict mypy on source, lenient on GPU/optional deps via overrides
- **GPU Handling:** All GPU imports deferred (imported in function bodies), tests run on CPU-only with mock injection pattern

     1→"""Adapter merging strategies for evolutionary combination.
     2→
     3→Implements TIES-Merging and DARE-Merging for combining multiple LoRA
     4→adapter state dicts into a single merged adapter. All GPU imports are
     5→deferred inside function bodies (INFRA-05 pattern).
     6→"""
     7→
     8→from __future__ import annotations
     9→
    10→from pathlib import Path
    11→from typing import Any
    12→
    13→
    14→def ties_merge(
    15→    state_dicts: list[dict[str, Any]],
    16→    density: float = 0.5,
    17→) -> dict[str, Any]:
    18→    """Merge adapter state dicts using TIES-Merging.
    19→
    20→    Trim-Elect-Sign-Disjoint merge: for each parameter, trims values
    21→    below density threshold, elects the majority sign (ignoring trimmed
    22→    values), then averages only the values matching the elected sign.
    23→
    24→    Args:
    25→        state_dicts: List of state dicts (tensors) to merge.
    26→        density: Fraction of values to keep per parameter (0.0 to 1.0).
    27→
    28→    Returns:
    29→        Merged state dict with same keys and shapes as inputs.
    30→
    31→    Raises:
    32→        ValueError: If *state_dicts* is empty or *density* is outside
    33→            ``[0.0, 1.0]``.
    34→    """
    35→    if not state_dicts:
    36→        raise ValueError(
    37→            "state_dicts must not be empty; TIES-merge of zero inputs is undefined."
    38→        )
    39→    if not (0.0 <= density <= 1.0):
    40→        raise ValueError(f"density must be between 0.0 and 1.0, got {density}")
    41→
    42→    import torch
    43→
    44→    merged: dict[str, Any] = {}
    45→    keys = state_dicts[0].keys()
    46→
    47→    for key in keys:
    48→        tensors = [sd[key].float() for sd in state_dicts]
    49→        stacked = torch.stack(tensors)
    50→
    51→        # Track which values survived trimming (True = kept)
    52→        keep_mask = torch.ones_like(stacked, dtype=torch.bool)
    53→
    54→        # Trim: zero out values below density threshold per tensor
    55→        for i in range(len(tensors)):
    56→            flat = stacked[i].abs().flatten()
    57→            if flat.numel() == 0:
    58→                continue
    59→            k_keep = max(1, int(flat.numel() * density))
    60→            threshold = torch.topk(flat, k_keep).values[-1]
    61→            mask = stacked[i].abs() >= threshold
    62→            keep_mask[i] = mask
    63→            stacked[i] = stacked[i] * mask.float()
    64→
    65→        # Elect sign: majority vote using only surviving (non-trimmed) values
    66→        signs = torch.sign(stacked)
    67→        signs_masked = signs * keep_mask.float()
    68→        sign_sum = signs_masked.sum(dim=0)
    69→        elected_sign = torch.sign(sign_sum)
    70→
    71→        # Where elected_sign == 0 (true tie), average all surviving values
    72→        tie_mask = elected_sign == 0
    73→
    74→        # Disjoint merge: average only values matching elected sign
    75→        matching = (signs == elected_sign.unsqueeze(0)).float()
    76→        # For tied positions, include all values that survived trimming
    77→        matching = torch.where(
    78→            tie_mask.unsqueeze(0),
    79→            keep_mask.float(),
    80→            matching,
    81→        )
    82→        matching_vals = stacked * matching
    83→        count = matching.sum(dim=0).clamp(min=1)
    84→        merged[key] = (matching_vals.sum(dim=0) / count).to(tensors[0].dtype)
    85→
    86→    return merged
    87→
    88→
    89→def dare_merge(
    90→    state_dicts: list[dict[str, Any]],
    91→    drop_rate: float = 0.1,
    92→) -> dict[str, Any]:
    93→    """Merge adapter state dicts using DARE-Merging.
    94→
    95→    Drop-And-REscale merge: randomly drops a fraction of values from
    96→    each state dict, then averages the remaining values with rescaling.
    97→
    98→    Args:
    99→        state_dicts: List of state dicts to merge.
   100→        drop_rate: Fraction of values to drop per parameter. Must be in
   101→            ``[0.0, 1.0)``.
   102→
   103→    Returns:
   104→        Merged state dict with same keys and shapes as inputs.
   105→
   106→    Raises:
   107→        ValueError: If *state_dicts* is empty or *drop_rate* is outside
   108→            ``[0.0, 1.0)``.
   109→    """
   110→    if not state_dicts:
   111→        raise ValueError(
   112→            "state_dicts must not be empty; DARE-merge of zero inputs is undefined."
   113→        )
   114→    if not (0.0 <= drop_rate < 1.0):
   115→        raise ValueError(f"drop_rate must be in [0.0, 1.0), got {drop_rate}")
   116→
   117→    import torch
   118→
   119→    merged: dict[str, Any] = {}
   120→    keys = state_dicts[0].keys()
   121→    scale = 1.0 / (1.0 - drop_rate)
   122→
   123→    for key in keys:
   124→        tensors = [sd[key].float() for sd in state_dicts]
   125→        stacked = torch.stack(tensors)
   126→
   127→        # Drop and rescale each tensor
   128→        for i in range(len(tensors)):
   129→            if drop_rate > 0:
   130→                mask = (torch.rand_like(stacked[i]) >= drop_rate).float()
   131→                stacked[i] = stacked[i] * mask * scale
   132→
   133→        # Average across state dicts
   134→        merged[key] = stacked.mean(dim=0).to(tensors[0].dtype)
   135→
   136→    return merged
   137→
   138→
   139→def load_adapter_state_dict(adapter_path: str | Path) -> dict[str, Any]:
   140→    """Load a LoRA adapter state dict from a safetensors file.
   141→
   142→    Args:
   143→        adapter_path: Path to the adapter .safetensors file.
   144→
   145→    Returns:
   146→        State dict mapping parameter names to tensors.
   147→    """
   148→    from safetensors.torch import load_file
   149→
   150→    return load_file(str(adapter_path), device="cpu")
   151→
```

   251→    def get_lineage(self, adapter_id: str) -> list[str]:
   252→        """Walk the parent_ids chain for an adapter.
   253→
   254→        Args:
   255→            adapter_id: Starting adapter ID.
   256→
   257→        Returns:
   258→            List of adapter IDs from the given adapter back to the root.
   259→        """
   260→        chain: list[str] = [adapter_id]
   261→        current_id = adapter_id
   262→        visited: set[str] = {adapter_id}
   263→
   264→        while True:
   265→            with Session(self._engine, expire_on_commit=False) as session:
   266→                record = session.get(AdapterRecord, current_id)
   267→                if record is None or record.parent_ids is None:
   268→                    break
   269→                parent_list: list[str] = json.loads(record.parent_ids)
   270→                if not parent_list:
   271→                    break
   272→                next_id = parent_list[0]
   273→                if next_id in visited:
   274→                    break
   275→                visited.add(next_id)
   276→                chain.append(next_id)
   277→                current_id = next_id
   278→
   279→        return chain
   280→

   282→        """Return adapters that have not been evaluated (pass_rate IS NULL).
   283→

   285→            task_type: Optional task type filter.

   287→        Returns:

    63→class D2LTrainConfig(BaseModel):
    64→    """Pydantic model for D2L training hyperparameters.
    65→
    66→    Enables validation, JSON serialization (for checkpoint storage), and
    67→    `.model_dump()` for MLflow experiment logging.
    68→
    69→    Attributes:
    70→        base_model_name: HuggingFace model name for the student/teacher base.
    71→        sakana_checkpoint_path: Path to the Sakana hypernet checkpoint.
    72→        num_steps: Total training steps.
    73→        lr: Learning rate for AdamW optimizer.
    74→        alpha: Blending weight for KL vs CE loss (1.0 = pure KL, 0.0 = pure CE).
    75→        temperature: Softmax temperature for KL divergence computation.
    76→        checkpoint_every: Steps between lightweight checkpoint saves.
    77→        full_checkpoint_every: Steps between full checkpoint saves (incl. optimizer).
    78→        checkpoint_dir: Directory for checkpoint output.
    79→        experiment_name: MLflow experiment name.
    80→        dry_run: If True, validate tensor shapes then exit.
    81→        smoke_test: If True, run 5 steps and verify loss trend.
    82→        dataset_path: Path to training JSONL file (required for full training).
    83→        grad_clip: Gradient clipping max norm.
    84→        warmup_steps: Number of linear LR warmup steps.
    85→        lora_r: LoRA rank.
    86→        max_length: Maximum tokenizer sequence length.
    87→    """
    88→
    89→    base_model_name: str = Field(default="Qwen/Qwen3-Coder-Next")
    90→    sakana_checkpoint_path: str
    91→    num_steps: int = Field(default=100)
    92→    lr: float = Field(default=2e-4)
    93→    alpha: float = Field(default=0.5)
    94→    temperature: float = Field(default=2.0)
    95→    checkpoint_every: int = Field(default=100)
    96→    full_checkpoint_every: int = Field(default=500)
    97→    checkpoint_dir: str = Field(default="./checkpoints")
    98→    experiment_name: str = Field(default="d2l-qwen3")
    99→    dry_run: bool = Field(default=False)
   100→    smoke_test: bool = Field(default=False)
   101→    dataset_path: str | None = Field(default=None)
   102→    grad_clip: float = Field(default=1.0)
   103→    warmup_steps: int = Field(default=10)
   104→    lora_r: int = Field(default=8)
   105→    max_length: int = Field(default=512)
   106→
   107→    @field_validator("lr")
   108→    @classmethod
   109→    def _validate_lr(cls, v: float) -> float:
   110→        """Ensure learning rate is strictly positive."""
   111→        if v <= 0:
   112→            raise ValueError(f"lr must be > 0, got {v}")
   113→        return v
   114→
   115→    @field_validator("alpha")

   120→            raise ValueError(f"alpha must be in [0, 1], got {v}")
   121→        return v
   122→
   123→    @field_validator("temperature")

   126→        """Ensure temperature is strictly positive."""
   127→        if v <= 0:
   128→            raise ValueError(f"temperature must be > 0, got {v}")
   129→        return v
   130→
   131→    @field_validator("num_steps")

   134→        """Ensure num_steps is strictly positive."""
   135→        if v <= 0:
   136→            raise ValueError(f"num_steps must be > 0, got {v}")
   137→        return v
   138→
   139→
   140→def _compute_kl_ce_loss(
   141→    student_logits: Any,
   142→    teacher_logits: Any,
   143→    answer_start: int,
   144→    config: D2LTrainConfig,
   145→) -> tuple[Any, dict[str, float]]:
   146→    """Compute blended KL-divergence and cross-entropy loss over the answer span.
   147→
   148→    Slices both logit tensors to ``[:, answer_start:, :]`` so that loss is
   149→    computed only on answer-span tokens (context tokens are masked out).
   150→
   151→    Args:
   152→        student_logits: Student model logits of shape (batch, seq_len, vocab).
   153→        teacher_logits: Teacher model logits of shape (batch, seq_len, vocab).
   154→        answer_start: Token index where the answer span begins.  Tokens before
   155→            this index are excluded from loss computation.
   156→        config: Training configuration supplying alpha and temperature.
   157→
   158→    Returns:
   159→        A tuple of:
   160→        - total_loss: Blended scalar tensor ``alpha * kl + (1 - alpha) * ce``.
   161→        - metrics: Dict with keys ``kl_loss``, ``ce_loss``, ``total_loss``
   162→          (Python floats, suitable for MLflow logging).
   163→    """
   164→    import torch.nn.functional as functional  # noqa: PLC0415
   165→
   166→    alpha = config.alpha
   167→    temp = config.temperature
   168→
   169→    # Slice to answer span only
   170→    s = student_logits[:, answer_start:, :]
   171→    t = teacher_logits[:, answer_start:, :]
   172→
   173→    vocab = s.shape[-1]
   174→

   100→def _ties_merge_adapters(
   101→    adapter_ids: list[str],
   102→    task_type: str,
   103→    registry: "AdapterRegistry",
   104→) -> str:
   105→    """Load adapter state dicts, TIES-merge them, and register the result.
   106→
   107→    All GPU imports are deferred (INFRA-05 pattern).
   108→
   109→    Args:
   110→        adapter_ids: IDs of adapters to merge.
   111→        task_type: Task type for the merged adapter.
   112→        registry: Registry for loading adapter paths and storing result.
   113→
